The Commissions say that the Commission is obliged to first examine whether the bill or proposals referred to them are contrary to the text or the spirit of the Constitution. If a commission sees that a bill or proposal is unconstitutional, it will reject it before it can negotiate its provisions. Now, for once, there's no hierarchical connection between commissions here. tbmm-2016-06-27sit06spe0416par0010	Thank you, Mr. President. We're discussing an important change and transformation of the judicial system. It is useful not to assess it through cheap polemics and bloated fears, but through the litigation courts that will be operational within a few days. Basically, it's not a stranger to our litigation system. With the modernisation of the Tanzimatic era, it was removed in 1924 on the grounds that the republic was unable to provide the expected benefit and prolong the work. The reason for not being able to provide the expected benefit is because there are not enough grown-up lawyers. However, the idea of inter-section between the first degree and the appeals has always held its place on the agenda. Talks at the opening of the judicial court, scientific articles, and drafts in the past that have not had a chance to pass the law are documents that present the trails of these debates. Dear lawmakers, it is possible to collect problems that have arisen and remain unresolved by over-centralization caused by our double-level judicial system: One is that the higher courts of the bilateral system have moved the higher courts to case control alongside legal supervision and have removed them from their original duties, the goal of being a court of law to lead to the development of law. Moreover, a limited inspection of the evidence collected by the first-degree courts has never produced satisfactory results in the study and discovery of material truth. Secondly, over-centralization has brought with it a heavy workload issue in terms of high courts and has affected the public's access to justice. In the past period, such negative influences have been forced to resort to capacity-enhancing measures, and they have shown positive incentives to ease the pressure of workload. It is clear that access to justice is the most rational cure for the balanced management of the labour burden and for access to justice. The third and perhaps most important consequence of over-centralization is the issue of judicial guarantees. What the Constitution protects and guarantees is not title or membership status, but basically a guarantee of judgment. There is no constitutional norm on the mandate of members, except for the heads of the Supreme Court, the vice presidents, the Attorney Generals, the Attorney Generals and the office heads. What is fundamental is that as long as the judicial guarantee is maintained, there is no obstacle to making legal arrangements on matters that the Constitution does not directly regulate and under the discretion of the lawmaker. In fact, the Constitution maintains the guarantee of justice and does not consider the court a possession of the woman. If the judicial titles and authorities that the constitutional norms don't grant a direct protection are granted, even at the level of first-degree courts, there's no way that HSYK will be able to maintain appreciation and saving respect for the chief prosecutors, commissioners, tribunal heads and members. The sub, upper distinction, which should not be understood in the form of this hierarchyal relationship, is also among the first-level courts. In this rating, the judge in the higher court is more senior, but he is able to oversee the judges' decisions in the lower court, and the appointment of judges in different-level courts does not mean that they should ever limit a lower or higher degree. While the relevant articles of the Constitution have brought a minimum measure in the selection of high court members, it has not stipulated that the qualifications of the members and the electoral procedures should be regulated by the law and limiting its mandate. At the beginning of the objections to the bill, you were going to launch a list of advocacy, referring to past-term regulations, and why did you increase the number of apartments and members in high courts earlier? The question is: It's useful to assess this in terms of measures to make it impossible to get out of the business load. A backward assessment will actually prove that these decisions are correct. As the postponement of the exceptions until today has come to the agenda based on practical and mandatory reasons, the capacity-enhancing measures that have been criticised today are also due to practical and mandatory needs. Another objection is based on the fact that after the bill is legalized, it will keep first-class bench judges with the condition to be elected high court members outside the source pool, and it is alleged to be contrary to the Constitutional decree on the ability to be elected. The relevant Article of the Constitution has predicted that the members of the Supreme Court can be elected between the first-class judicial judges and the prosecutors of the republic and those considered to be members of this profession. However, the last joke of the same article has left the quality and selection of members to the law. Because of his authority to establish constitutional qualifications to the lawmaker, there is no validity to the Constitutional claim of violation of the Constitution.
